Player_White (to Proxy):

The position on the board is a typical middlegame arising from the exchange variation of the Ruy Lopez. 

### Position Analysis:
* **Pawn Structure**: Black has doubled c-pawns ($c7$ and $c6$), giving White a healthier pawn structure and a long-term endgame advantage. However, Black possesses the bishop pair as compensation, with the light-squared bishop currently active on $c4$ and the dark-squared bishop on $d6$.
* **The Pin**: Our bishop on $g5$ exerts useful pressure by pinning the knight on $f6$.
* **Our Plan**: We want to mobilize our pieces, activate our rooks, and prepare thematic knight maneuvers towards the $f5$ outpost (e.g., $Nh4 \rightarrow Nf5$ or $Nd2 \rightarrow Nf1 \rightarrow Ne3 \rightarrow Nf5$). 

### The Move: `d2e3` (Qe3)
By moving the queen to $e3$, we achieve several positional goals:
1. **Rook Activation**: We clear the $d1$-rook's line of sight, allowing it to command the semi-open $d$-file.
2. **Central Control**: The queen on $e3$ is highly active, reinforcing our $e4$-pawn and eyeing potential weaknesses on the $a7$-$g1$ diagonal.
3. **Knight Maneuvering**: We pave the way for our $f3$-knight or a $Nd2$ maneuver without blocking our queen, while preparing to park a knight on the critical $f5$ square.
